Title: The Christian History, containing accounts of the revival and propagation of religion in Great-Britain and America; the bound volume of weekly numbers published by Thomas Prince, junior, Boston, 1743-1744
Creator: Thomas Prince, junior (1722-1748), publisher; the letters and accounts are their writers' own (ministers of New England, Scotland and England)
Publisher: Boston: printed by S. Kneeland and T. Green for T. Prince, junior
Publication Date: 1744
Rights: Public Domain (published 1744, more than 95 years ago; public domain by date under the project's rolling rule; the item's own rights statement reads 'NOT_IN_COPYRIGHT')
Source: Internet Archive item christianhistor00unkngoog (https://archive.org/details/christianhistor00unkngoog), the item's own christianhistor00unkngoog_djvu.txt full-text OCR derivative; contributing library per the item metadata: Oxford University; host metadata date: 1744. Fetched directly (curl/urllib, raw bytes) by the Library thread (Era VIII pass), 2026-10-23. sha256 of the raw fetched bytes: 4432cc8165fbf3694da1b502ab2608967700cf7b52c9883eb1718943c352c5cb.
Provenance note: Header prepended by the Library thread; the host's front matter up to and including the line beginning 'at |http: //books' removed (47 lines); nothing else altered, no OCR error corrected, nothing retyped. Google Books scan from the Bodleian; the Google notice at the head of the file is removed as recorded above; the early pages are garbled, the body readable with the long s rendered as f.
Content note: The revival's own weekly magazine, the first religious periodical in America: ministers' letters on the awakenings in their towns, accounts from Scotland and England, and reprinted Puritan testimonies; the census-named serialised self-documentation of the movement.

138 Mr. Corbet of the Enmity of 

Moreover true Chriftianity is a Light, by which all Tilings 
that are reproved, are made manifeft ; and the World that 
lives in Sin and loves Darknefs, hates the Lights by which 
It is condemned, not only with an Hatred of Averfation,: 
but of hoftile Perfecution. 

* Neverthelefs, the unregenerate Sort of Men in general^ 
(Cannot and would not abandon all Senfe of Religion, which 
is fo deeply iinprinted in human Nature, and the Forni 
whereof may be acceptable even to corrupt N*.ture. Where- 
fore they own the Name, while they hate the Thing j and 
keep up a Shew and Form thereof, while they deny the 
Truth and Power. And having a falfe Apprehenfion of 
Chrift they adore him, while they trample upon his prefent 
Members, that really bear his Image ; and having a falfe 
Idea of Godlinefs, they honour the Memory of the Saints 
of former Ages, while they vilify thofe of their own Times, 
in whomGodlinefs really cxifts : which (hews, that if Chrift: 
and the former Saints were now on the Earth to appear 
what they were indeed, they would be no lefs hated and- 
icorned than the Faithful that are now living. 

* The manifold Waysof Deftruftion and Mifery whereinE 
the Wicked walk, tho' contradi<ftory to each other, do alt 
confpire in this Enmity ; and Godlinefs is put to confli6t 
not with one Sort of Enemies, but with the various corrupt: 
Parties of the divided carnal World. Yet worldly Interefts: 
often make their Advantages of Chriftianity, and have their 
Defigns upon it, and complicate themfelves with it in {bme= 
external and accidental Engagements for a Seafcn, and then^ 
the Enmity is reftrainedr And not a few, that fall fhort of 
Regeneration, may be fo illuminated and wrought upon, as 
not only to ceafe from Malignancy and Hoftility againft it,

of hearty Mourners of all Orders and every Denomination. 
The Memqry of the Just is blessed.
